│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │

│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │

│                                                                              │

│  • Based on the provided task by the user, use the given tools to gather     │
│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[solver]'.                                           │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │

│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │

│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Tester agent of the Multi Agent System.                 │
│  • The given task is already solved by the previous agents.                  │
│  • Your task is now to test if the overall user's task was completed         │
│    successfully.                                                             │
│  • Use the information provided by previous agents, along with your          │
│    available tools, to test the solution.                                    │
│  • If you come to the conclusion that the task is not solved successfully    │
│    and entirely, describe concisely why it is not solved and include the tag │
│    '[solver]'.                                                               │
│  • If the task is solved successfully write a summary of what you did and    │
│    include the tag '[approver]'.                                             │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

╭──────────────────── logging - approver - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[solver]'.                                       │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│ ┃    invoking make, which loses things like -fPIC. So... we avoid using    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃     it directly and instead add a level of indirection.  We combine      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃     $(CFLAGS) and $(CPPFLAGS) here because that's the way the legacy     ┃ │

│ ┃     build did it and many builds rely on that. See main.mk for more      ┃ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   configure-time, and expanded them into the generated Makefile. There   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    are, in that build, no uses of CPPFLAGS in the configure-expanded     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    Makefile. Ergo: if a client configures with CPPFLAGS=... and then     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃        explicitly passes CFLAGS=... to make, the CPPFLAGS will be        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                lost. That behavior is retained in 3.48+.                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ instrumentation\n\n- Goal: build SQLite from the provided vendor snapshot    │
│ and make an instrumented sqlite3 CLI available on PATH.\n- Source used:      │

│ Build plan: run the shipped configure with --gcov (and --prefix=/usr/local), │
│ make, then install the resulting sqlite3 into a PATH directory.\n-           │
│ Execution:\n  - cd /app/sqlite; ./configure --gcov --prefix=/usr/local\n  -  │
│ make -j$(nproc)\n  - make install (and a manual install of                   │
│ /app/sqlite/sqlite3 → /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 when needed)\n- Key build       │
│ details found:\n  - configure --gcov enables coverage: adds                  │
│ -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST=1 -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage to CFLAGS and links  │
│ with -lgcov (USE_GCOV in the generated Makefile).\n  - Configure will build  │
│ the in-tree jimsh if no system tcl is present, so source generation works    │
│ without extra deps.\n- Results / verification:\n  - sqlite3 binary installed │
│ at /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 and on PATH (which sqlite3 →                       │
│ /usr/local/bin/sqlite3).\n  - /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 --version reported the  │
│ expected build (3.50.4 ...).\n  - Compile-time coverage artifacts (.gcno)    │
│ created in /app/sqlite (e.g. sqlite3.gcno, sqlite3-shell.gcno).\n  - Running │
│ the instrumented sqlite3 produced runtime coverage files (.gcda) in          │
│ /app/sqlite.\n  - Built libraries (libsqlite3.so, libsqlite3.a) are present  │
│ in the source dir; installing them to /usr/local/lib (and running ldconfig   │
│ or updating LD_LIBRARY_PATH) is optional.\n- Lessons / notes:\n  - configure │
│ --gcov is sufficient to produce gcov-instrumented objects; to generate       │
│ coverage reports run the instrumented binary (or tests) to create .gcda      │
│ files and use gcov/gcovr to process .gcno/.gcda.\n  - If you prefer a        │
│ different install location, rerun configure with --prefix (e.g.,             │
│ --prefix=/app) so all outputs are installed under one directory.\n\nStatus:  │
│ Completed — instrumented sqlite3 built from the vendored source, coverage    │
│ artifacts generated, and sqlite3 placed on PATH.'                            │

│ Summary — SQLite build with gcov instrumentation                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Goal: build SQLite from the provided vendor snapshot and make an          │
│    instrumented sqlite3 CLI available on PATH.                               │
│  • Source used: /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz → extracted to      │
│    /app/sqlite.                                                              │
│  • Build plan: run the shipped configure with --gcov (and                    │
│    --prefix=/usr/local), make, then install the resulting sqlite3 into a     │
│    PATH directory.                                                           │
│  • Execution:                                                                │
│     • cd /app/sqlite; ./configure --gcov --prefix=/usr/local                 │
│     • make -j$(nproc)                                                        │
│     • make install (and a manual install of /app/sqlite/sqlite3 →            │
│       /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 when needed)                                    │
│  • Key build details found:                                                  │
│     • configure --gcov enables coverage: adds -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST=1       │
│       -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage to CFLAGS and links with -lgcov         │
│       (USE_GCOV in the generated Makefile).                                  │
│     • Configure will build the in-tree jimsh if no system tcl is present, so │
│       source generation works without extra deps.                            │
│  • Results / verification:                                                   │
│     • sqlite3 binary installed at /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 and on PATH (which  │
│       sqlite3 → /usr/local/bin/sqlite3).                                     │
│     • /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 --version reported the expected build (3.50.4   │
│       ...).                                                                  │
│     • Compile-time coverage artifacts (.gcno) created in /app/sqlite (e.g.   │
│       sqlite3.gcno, sqlite3-shell.gcno).                                     │
│     • Running the instrumented sqlite3 produced runtime coverage files       │
│       (.gcda) in /app/sqlite.                                                │
│     • Built libraries (libsqlite3.so, libsqlite3.a) are present in the       │
│       source dir; installing them to /usr/local/lib (and running ldconfig or │
│       updating LD_LIBRARY_PATH) is optional.                                 │
│  • Lessons / notes:                                                          │
│     • configure --gcov is sufficient to produce gcov-instrumented objects;   │
│       to generate coverage reports run the instrumented binary (or tests) to │
│       create .gcda files and use gcov/gcovr to process .gcno/.gcda.          │
│     • If you prefer a different install location, rerun configure with       │
│       --prefix (e.g., --prefix=/app) so all outputs are installed under one  │
│       directory.                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ Status: Completed — instrumented sqlite3 built from the vendored source,     │
│ coverage artifacts generated, and sqlite3 placed on PATH.                    │
